**Migration — Translation 5 (EventSchema 81; migrate-local, `internal/record/migrate`).** Three parts,
one change: (a) a Mint's `supersedes` is dropped where the ancestor is closed — no `"gap"."open"` —
on the destination at the successor's place (`dropClosedAncestorLink`, beside Translation 2's
`dropCrossSeatLink`, reading `q` as it does); an ancestor an earlier supersession closed is closed;
(b) an archived `Close`'s `successor` column is dropped by a registry entry, and `repaired_with_regression`
(and the round era's `closed_with_regression`, `eraentries.go:22`) is respelled `repaired` in
`valueRenames` — so Translation 4 still rewrites an archived bench ruling's word to its for-blue
`not_a_defect` after decoding; (c) an archived `amends_prior` has no mapping, so one would be refused
loudly: T-B43 asserts there is none. Measured 2026-11-23 over the 34 SQLite archives (§II.4's row): 18
closes carry a successor, every one after a mint superseding its own gap, so `actOnSupersededGap`
already writes each as no event where Translation 2 keeps the link (quadratic-formula's merge seats
are the chair's after translation, so its 7 keep theirs); 12 own-seat links name an ancestor a
`close` row ended first (bench-closed ancestors not counted); 0 `amends_prior`. Residue, stated: each
dropped link, with `supersedes_count`, its place in `successorsOf`, its passed demand (none measured:
no archived non-self `demand_lineage` row) and its successor's lineage in the report; the dropped
`successor` values; blue engagements the dropped links orphan (`UnowedEngagements`). The 6 August JSONL
runs are not measured here; T-B43 counts every archive. The archived `estoppel` log rows (2) translate
as `entries.go:208-210` writes them today. Whether a dropped link or a respelled close leaves a later
archived act refused (the cascade Translation 2 had, R10) is NOT MEASURED until T-B43 runs at B7; the
replay places estoppel `Tombstone` rows from the
archived verbatim edits as §III.B12 does; migrate runs no `requireNotEstopped` (a verb guard), so no
archived mint is re-judged under the per-seat rule.

**Tests** (driven through the command tree as seats run it):

- **T-B41** `TestSupersedesNamesOnlyYourOwnOpenGap` (`internal/cli`): lens A mints G; A's mint
  `--supersedes G` lands and closes G; lens B's mint `--supersedes` of A's open H is refused naming H's
  assignee; A's and B's mint `--supersedes` of a closed gap (closed by `close`, by a ruling, by an
  earlier supersession) are each refused with the closed-ancestor text; `lens close --superseded-by`
  is an unknown flag; `--as repaired_with_regression` and `--as amends_prior` are refused by the enum on
  `close`, `closing-blue` and `motion docket file close`; the generated `schema.sql` holds no
  `successor` column on `close` or `motion_close` (field 7 is reserved, so no body can carry it). Fails if any closed-ancestor link lands, a successor field survives, or either word does.
- **T-B42** `TestEstoppelBindsOnlyThePrescribingLens` (`internal/cli`): lens P mints G with a verified
  fix; blue applies it verbatim (`blue edit --accept`); P's mint quoting the applied text is refused,
  the `estoppel` log entry written and its text naming no `--supersedes`; P's mint `--supersedes G`
  quoting it is refused too (the exemption is gone); lens Q's mint quoting it, of another class, lands;
  of G's class on G's sentence while G is open it is refused by the one-gap rule, and lands once P
  closes G; Q's closing road quoting the text lands where P's is refused (L43); Q's refuting standing
  reading of the sentence keeps its debt until Q mints. A ruling tombstone on G refuses both P's and Q's
  same-class mint. Fails without the seat argument: Q's mint is refused as today.
- **T-B43** `TestNoArchiveKeepsALinkPastAClose` (`internal/record/migrate`): every archived run
  replays with zero refusals, zero `Mint.supersedes` naming a gap closed at the successor's place, zero
  `Close.successor`, zero `repaired_with_regression` or `amends_prior`, and the manifest's Translation 5
  counts equal to the dropped links and respelled closes by source id per run (the measured figures
  above are its expectation where the replay reaches them). Fails if a translation keeps a link past a
  close or an archive holds a word it does not map.
- T-B14 is narrowed (§III.B4a); T-B11's R2-7 `demand_lineage` report and T-B16's lineage reach are
  deleted with the table; T-B35 (§III.B14) loses its L13 case.

**Release fuzz.** The regression-close and `amends_prior` drives go; `mintEstopped` drives the
prescribing lens and a new drive mints another lens's gap on the same applied text, counted under a
new gate key `estoppel_other_lens_admitted`; the `estoppels` gate keeps its key, fed by the prescribing
lens. blue's `engage` gate stays, fed by a lens's own supersede of its open gap in the sitting before
blue's; a zero there is a gap in that drive, fixed in the drive, never by deleting the key (R34).

now carries, each tagged PROPOSAL-STANDS | DOCKETING-STANDS | DELETE | TEXT (§III.B13); **M17** (amendment 2,

DELETE | TEXT, and the SIDED readers (amendment 4; amendment 3's REJECT-MOVES, §III.B14); **M18** (amendment 3, audit p7 gap 1) every reader of a
docket ruling's gap or word, 110 hits tagged DOCKETING-KEY | DOCKETING-STANDS | PROPOSAL | PETITION | DELETE;

rows, §III.B6). Generated carriers (edit the source, regenerate, `-check` clean): `protogen`,

check 5 leaves only the appendix's O lines.

## IV. Risk & Mitigation

| # | Risk | L×I×C | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | protogen/decode/planguard cannot carry a body nested as a filing arm | **hit** (spike, 2026-11-17) | **Resolved by the amendment** (act on the motion, picked 2026-11-17): the act is a second oneof of `Motion`, a shape protogen, the decode, `insertBody` and `classify` already carry; B1 re-proves it by the decode round trip (`TestEveryBodyRoundTripsThroughTheGeneratedDecode` reaches each act arm) and places an arm's list in `migrate`'s `identityBody` |
| R2 | The verb's write function is factored but a guard stays in the verb's `RunE` | M×H×L | T-B5 per effect; delete-the-row on each M10 effect |
| R3 | A ruling for either side, or a grade answer, reads as unruled somewhere (`grademovement.go:90` restarts its window at the latest grade ruling, so an unruled-reading `--keep` re-dockets the movement) | M×H×L | `rulingWord`'s side and grade arms; M11 census; T-B10; delete-the-row |
| R4 | A bench ruling lands outside a sitting convened for its docketing, a held proposal is not answered, or an exit ruling is refused and `bench outcome` stalls | M×H×L | one predicate, `benchHeld`, on every path (§III.B5); T-B9 a row per path; oracle `bench_ruled_unheld`; gate `bench_answers_by_path` |
| R5 | Two answers on one M | L×H×L | in-transaction `requireUnanswered`; T-B7; oracle |
| R6 | The successor's assignee diverges between the view and a stream reader; an assignee reader keeps the event seat, or an acting reader reads the wrong seat | M×M×L | one stored column, the rule rendered by one function; the writer is the event's seat everywhere (amendment 7 deletes `actingSeat`); M9 tags every reader ASSIGNEE \| WRITER; T-B14 runs every row on the assignee's own successor (amendment 8: no other kind lands, and `assigneeSQL` is the writer's seat, L57); oracle `assignee_drift` recomputes the assignee independently |
| R10 | The migrate translation cascades: a dropped link or a dropped engagement leaves a later archived act refused | M×M×L | the 20 acts and 3 engagements are measured (B5; the plan's 11 and 2 counted 36 archives) (§II.4, `supersede-a/acts.txt`); T-B11 asserts each act present; T-B13 zero refusals; a further refusal stops phase B5 and goes to gblock as residue (§III.B7) |
| R11 | A successor (since amendment 8 only its writer's own amendment of an open gap) lands with an assignee that does not act on it: outside its area, or no longer cast | M×M×L | no reader keys on area (§III.B4a, T-B16); an uncast assignee owes no closing and the gap goes as any uncast assignee's does today (`closingowed.go:79`); `lineage_successors_reowned` and `assignee_drift` in the fuzz |
| R9 | `gap_refresh` reads `gap_state` (the ancestor's row) inside the flush's `INSERT OR REPLACE INTO "gap_state"` | L×M×L | **Moot after amendment 8** (L57: `assigneeSQL` reads no `gap_state` row). Before it: SQLite evaluates the source before the replace; the predecessor is never in flight in its successor's mint append. T-B14's state steps; `TestEveryGapMarkerIsNeeded` re-run; the compile guard's x1.2 bound (#1811) |
| R7 | A frozen gap stalls when nobody accepts | L×M×L | the assignee's window is one sitting; its end writes `Docketing{PROPOSAL}`, whose filer's side lands at every path: a close as filed, a regrade or the other side's grades, and a supersede whose successor was checked mintable against G's assignee at the filing, its inputs frozen to the ruling (§III.B13, §III.B14's sweep table, L48; T-B38) |
| R24 | A proposal filed before a non-remand ruling of its gap is answered after it, overturning the ruling (double jeopardy) | M×H×L | none can be: a proposal is refused while a docketing stands (L38), and a condition docketing is refused while a proposal stands (L28), so every proposal standing at a ruling is the one it answers; T-B30; oracle `proposal_outlived_ruling` |
| R25 | A reader of a docket ruling resolves its gap through the motion and reads an empty gap for a ruling that names none (a supersession fold that closes nothing, a LEAD line with no gap), or the docket-order audit reads motions and returns a clean zero | M×H×L | one Go function `DocketRulingGap` and the docketings fold; appendix M18 tags every hit of the reader sweep; T-B32 (each reader, live and migrated), T-B33 (non-zero expected) |
| R26 | A side's statement is lost or misread (amendment 4): a for-blue answer writes a word blue's closing did not state (other than the `not_a_defect` default), a word lands on blue's closing where its proposal is its statement, a `sided_with` outside D's pair lands, or a reader of "the held act applied" keys on the old `accepted` and reads every ruling as unapplied | M×H×L | validate's `DocketRuling` arm checks the pair and the word against blue's closing; the closing verb refuses a word off `IMPASSE`; one stored field, `sided_with`, whose readers M17's SIDED tag lists (`sided_with = BLUE` for "applied", amendment 7); T-B27, T-B35, T-B10; gate `blue_word_applied`, oracle `side_stated_nothing` |
| R27 | A `Close` ends a docketing it should not, or one end arm lands without the other: a carry ends an F8 docketing, a `Close` before D's row ends D, or the column and the fold disagree once P12b's moot close lands | M×M×L | the end reads a non-carry `Close` written after D's row (L42), in exactly two places; T-B34 compares column and fold after every step and carries the carry and before-D rows; P12b's admission of its moot close is its own (§VI Open) |
| R12 | A proposal ruled against is refiled unchanged, and the gap cycles assignee → bench → ruling against | M×M×L | that ruling places G's ruling tombstone row; `requireNotEstoppedOn` refuses every seat proposal on G while it stands; only blue's reword of G's sentence (an anchor gap: the anchor's reword or retire; an inquiry gap: its next fate) ends it, a cut none (§III.B12); T-B19 |

| R8 | #1828 lands in another shape, or not first | M×M×L | each DDL phase takes the branch's epoch + 1 at its build (L16); the build stops if `mintbudget.go` still exists |

| R19 | A duty the dispatch's motion carried is lost or kept on the motion when the docketing replaces it: closings never owed, a bench readied twice for one docketing, PASS allowed with a gap before the bench, a docketed gap left on a party's list | M×H×L | §III.B13's duty table, each row re-keyed by meaning; appendix M16 tags every reader PROPOSAL-STANDS or DOCKETING-STANDS; T-B23 a subtest per row; check 7 deletes each re-key |
| R20 | Translation 3 leaves an archived ruling naming no docketing, or an archived party docket with no act unreplayable | M×M×L | Translation 3 lands in B2a with validate's no-act refusal; O2 (ruled) drops the party- and hand-filed dockets, 16 (O5), and the 22 closings filed on them (O6), both confirmed 2026-11-20; T-B26; check 9 zero refusals |
| R21 | Red's text applied by a for-red answer is not the text red's closing was validated with — the report moved under it, the tool re-plans differently, or no text stands (missing closing, uncast assignee, an inquiry or section gap) and for-red lands anyway; or the applied edit is read as blue's act (an exchange counted, the ruling's tombstone ended by its own edit) | M×H×L | the closing is validated by `blue.PlanAccept`, the accept's own planner; the freeze refuses blue's edit or retire over the closing's span while D stands; validate compares the bench's edit with the ruling's re-plan; a for-red answer with no text is refused; the road is estopped as a mint is, at the closing and at the ruling; the freeze re-plans every standing road on blue's edited report (L45); the edit's seat is the bench and it precedes the ruling, ends before placements (M20); T-B29; gate `red_text_applied`, oracle `bench_edit_not_red_text` |
| R22 | The bench writes a word or grade of its own through a path the deletion missed, or the tool's word or restored grade differs from the row's; or grade movement leaves the window through a ruling that did not decide it | M×H×L | no bench flag or verb chooses either (M17 DELETE rows); validate's `DocketRuling` arm compares the word with the row's (`not_a_defect`, `repaired`, the stored word) and the bench `Regrade` with the stored or restored grades; the window restarts only at a grade-movement ruling, and per axis at a regrade proposal's ruling naming it (L41, L41′); T-B27, T-B28; oracle `bench_grade_of_its_own` |

## V. Phases and Verification Plan

**One change, built in phases, merged as a pair** (gblock 2026-11-14: "Build and commit incrementally.
Merge as a pair." and "Like commit to the pr so you don't lose your work / But they should merge
together"). Each phase is committed and pushed to the change's draft PR as it is built, green on the
full check (check 8) before the next starts; Part A's phases first, then Part B's; the change reaches
`main` in one merge — one PR, or two stacked PRs merged as a pair, the child retargeted to `main`
before its parent merges (AGENTS.md). `main` never holds Part A without Part B, so no interim road,
residue or release exists between them. The release fuzz (check 10) runs on the PR's final head.

T-A3's road text names `motion docket file regrade|close` for blue, and no proposal for a lens
(amendment 7, B7); before B7 the built text names the supersede proposal too.

Before the pair merges: an independent review of the built change, and the audit NOTES discharged or dismissed in the PR body.

**Validation loop** (each phase runs the checks its row names, then check 8). First `unset $(env | grep -o '^FEOV_[A-Z_]*')`,
`export GOTOOLCHAIN=local`; absolute paths; read the FAIL COUNT; `uptime` before believing a timeout.

1. **Proto committed.** `go -C scripts run ./protogen && git diff --exit-code -- '*record.pb.go' '*record.proto.sha256' '*decode_gen.go' '*body_gen.go'` → no diff. *Re-armed by:* `record.proto`.
2. **Epoch.** `go -C scripts run ./schemagen && grep -n eventSchema plugins/frank-exchange-of-views/requirements.json` → `requirements.json` and `record.EventSchema` agree, and the value is the phase's own: 78 from A1, 79 from B1, 80 from B2a, 81 from B7 (each the branch's epoch + 1 at its build, L16, L50); after a merge of `main`, greater than `main`'s; `schema.epoch.json` restamped with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` only in A1, B1, B2a and B7. *Re-armed by:* any event, table, column or view change.
3. **Carriers.** `go -C plugins/frank-exchange-of-views/tools run ./devcmd/vocabgen && go -C plugins/frank-exchange-of-views/tools run ./devcmd/cmdtreegen && go -C scripts run ./agentgen -check && go -C plugins/frank-exchange-of-views/tools run ./devcmd/enginegen -check && go -C scripts run ./vocabdoc && git status --short` → only the phase's carriers change. *Re-armed by:* help, terms, agentgen sources, proto options.

One `--smoke` run on the merge candidate: no `finding` table; every docket motion stores an act and none is the dispatch's, every answered motion has exactly one `MotionRule`, every bench docket ruling names a docketing, each docketing has at most one, no bench `Regrade` stands outside the transaction of a for-blue (for-filer) answer to a regrade proposal or of `--restore` at grade movement (`moved_grades: RESTORED`), every answer at impasse carries its side's statement (`repaired` after the bench's edit of red's closing's text, else blue's closing's word, else `not_a_defect`), every non-remand ruling but one on a grade leaves its gap closed and no later answer on that gap applies a statement a standing ruling refused (T-B36's property, recomputed), no for-red or for-assignee answer applies text no closing of that side carried, every docketing written at the `FinalSitting` plan had its closings readied before the terminal bench (a docketing at the final sitting holding none is NOT MEASURED, not a pass), no proposal was filed on a docketed gap, and each was written in a sitting `benchHeld` held it in, every gap's view `assignee` is its writer, or for a mint naming an ancestor that ancestor's `assignee`, no Mint names two gaps, every non-remand bench ruling is followed in its transaction by its gap's ruling `Tombstone` row, every docketing the plans named landed, and no seat mint or proposal landed under a standing tombstone. *Re-armed by:* the merge candidate.

## VI. Forks

### Ruled (each on its recommendation unless marked; option labels are agent wording; only gblock's typed words are quoted)

| # | Ruled | Since |
|---|---|---|
| F1 | A dispute by G's own assignee is refused; the assignee acts | 2026-11-12 |

Fewer mechanisms than fields on the acts: the ending acts span three bodies (`BlueEdit` `reopened`, `Retire`, `Inquiry`), so an end field on each is three child tables and indexes, and whether a bar stands becomes a join across them — the derived design again; and a tombstone field on `DocketRuling` or `BlueEdit` would restate the motion's gap or the edit's `answers`, which `record.proto`'s Motion comment ("A second field holding the same word could only agree with the arm or contradict it") rules out. No field holds an `events.id` (stable `gap` and `motion` instead), so the replay's id shift needs only `newID` taken from the id `Replay.Write` returns rather than `MAX("id")`, and a source row is written as no event, re-derived from its act (§III.B12, migrate-local). Its key is the seat's ordinal (`numbered`, as `Stale`), so place, end and place again never collide. ### Amendment 2026-11-20 (Bench convenes)

The plan was approved 2026-11-15 with the dispatch filing docket motions. gblock's ruling of
2026-11-18/20 (RULINGS, the bullet of those dates; evidence `~/.claude/scratch/1695/impasse-arch/EVIDENCE.md`)
makes a motion only a party's proposal and puts a gap before the bench by a tool-written
`Docketing{gap, cause}` row (§III.B13). The amendment rewrites D3, D5, D8, D9, adds D13, B13 and R19–R20,
re-keys §III.B3–B5, B7–B10, B12 and §IV–§V to the docketing, withdraws F7, L6 and L25, amends F3, F5 and
L7, and adds L27–L30 and phase B2a, which reshapes B1's record and B2's code at `main` + 3. Audit p6's
gap (accept and reject on a no-act motion) dissolves with the no-act motion: every docketing takes
accept or reject (amendment 2 deleted B13's "no proposal stands" refusal). A re-audit precedes phase B2a.

  sitting, admitting only that (§III.B14, L44′, T-B37).

  a new question, and red's text on S may be applied. Not taken: (b), a stored bar on the text G's
  ruling refused.
- **F-LT — picked "A ruling re-locks" (audit p9), replacing lead call (a); span picked "Ends with the

| # | Question | Options and consequences |
|---|---|---|
| F-CS | May a lens name **another lens's CLOSED gap** in `--supersedes`? | (a) **Yes, as today** (`requireSupersedable` admits any seat on a closed ancestor): the successor is the ancestor lens's (F-B3, `debate.js:910` "a successor you mint of another lens's closed gap is that lens's to audit and close"), so lens B's mint opens a gap lens A owns — a third party's act on A's chain, in tension with "only a gap's two parties" and with the ancestor ruling ("own-gap lineage"); the live-answer demand passes through the link; migrate's Translation 2 still drops the 16 archived links (L10 → all links), so live and archive differ. (b) **Own chain only**: `requireSupersedable` refuses an ancestor of another assignee, open or closed; B's failed-repair finding is B's own fresh gap (assignee B, no link, no demand passed); Translation 2 and the live rule agree. Under (b) estoppel's `--supersedes` escape (`estoppel.go:34-35`) becomes own-chain only, so a lens that finds a defect in text another lens prescribed and blue applied verbatim — the 2 archived estoppel refusals, U39 and U51, both obeyed by a cross-lens supersede of a closed gap — is barred outright: (b) needs L18 narrowed (estoppel binds only the prescribing lens) or that residue accepted. (c) Any lens, but the successor is the **minter's** (assignee = writer on a closed ancestor): keeps the 16 uses' lineage and the demand, ends the third-party assignment; reverses F-B3 for the closed case. The lead asked gblock 2026-11-23; F-B3, B4a's writer/assignee case, T-B14's S1 and `debate.js:910` move with the answer. |
| F-AR | Where does a lens's **argument about another lens's gap** go, and what of the **two defects no act records** (§III.B15: a same-class defect on another lens's open gap's subject, refused by the one-gap rule; a defect in another lens's verbatim-applied prescription while its gap is open, refused by estoppel and `requireSupersedable`)? (b) The voice case only: risk-matrix narration in G's text becomes a gap of the voice lens's own about the risk matrix's row (a new about-kind, which Part A's about-gap refusal forbids today). (c) A recorded argument with no act, read by G's lens on its work list (a new body; the finding by another name). (d) For the two unrecordable defects only: let the one-gap rule and estoppel bind the gap's own chain only (another lens mints its same-class gap; estoppel narrowed to the prescribing lens, L18 reversed) — two gaps of one class on one subject, which the one-gap rule exists to refuse. The lead recommends none over another: (a) loses real defects silently, which R31 names. |

(b) Kept for blue alone: keeps `Mint supersede = 17`, the stored successor, the bench `Mint`, `actingSeat` and all of L48 for one filer — most of X1–X41 stays. Recommendation: (a). |
| F-AP | Delete **`amends_prior`**? Raised, not ruled | 0 archived uses as a closure class (`successor-audit.md`); designed with BOTH amended ids in `supersedes` (`b91a1d4ee`), which the one-ancestor rule (§III.B11) cannot hold; nothing checks it since `06bcd0c54`. (a) Delete the value (reserved), its means text and readers. (b) Keep it with one ancestor, its second link lost. Not deleted by this amendment. |

**Amendment 7's concern answered — the 53 of 70 misstatement disputes are not residue.** Amendment 7
read "a lens that disagrees with how G states its defect argues and does not file" as leaving those
disputes with no road (R31). Under amendment 8 each has one: G's own lens amends its open gap
(`--supersedes` is that act, and the archive shows it used so 21 times, 17 voice-driven), and where
G's lens does not, blue's word — `not_a_defect` in its close proposal, or its closing at G's impasse —
puts the dispute before the bench, which sides. A third lens does nothing while G is open and mints its
own gap after G closes if the defect remains; the misstatement in G's own text stops printing at G's
close (the risk matrix prints open gaps only).

**Filed, outside #1889's scope:** #1930 (premature or narrow closure — closure strength) and #1931
(an inquiry re-fate undoes a closure with no link to the gap). Neither is a dependency of B7.

**Measured for the answer above (`~/.claude/scratch/1695/amend8/bin/measure3.py`):** of the 35
own-seat links on an open ancestor in the SQLite archives, 0 were minted after a verbatim-applied edit
answering that ancestor, so no archived own amendment would meet the estoppel bar that loses its
`--supersedes` escape.

**Not closed by the rulings, stated for gblock:** the prescribing lens has no road on its own
verbatim-applied text when the prescription itself is the defect (PRESCRIPTION-WRONG: 9 archived, 4
found by the prescribing lens itself — `merge` ×2, voice U74, U75 — and 8 of the 9 after the
ancestor's close): while its gap is open, its own `--supersedes` quoting that text is estopped (the
escape is deleted), and once it is closed the text stands for it; only another lens can raise it. The
plan builds the ruling as written (T-B42 pins it). Further calls are the lead's, not ruled: L55
deletes `demand_lineage` on the task brief's instruction and so reverses the 2026-11-05 "Supersede
demand" ruling for own amendments; L56 deletes the repair-regression telemetry; L57 and L58 follow
from the rulings' plain reading.
